- 2026-07-11 | ER-109 Morning Brief merge-gate round 7 (governor handshake `5cf8866d`) — closed the exact-midnight expiry hole the governor confirmed on the post-round-6 tree (`0a4b62f`): `valid_until` was only a "stay fresh until" window, so at `now == valid_until` the `now < valid_until` fresh check was false and the verdict fell through to the poll-cadence age ladder — a brief composed 23:59 (valid to next midnight) read `fresh` AT midnight and `aging` at 00:05 instead of `stale`. Fix: a present `valid_until` is now a HARD expiry on BOTH the Python (`mission_control_common.feed_health`) and rendered-JS (`dashboard/index.html` `guard`) sides — `now >= valid_until` ⇒ `state=stale`, `red=True` immediately, inserted before the fresh-window branch so the existing far-future/47h/30h horizon negatives (all `now < valid_until`) fall through unchanged. Before/after synthetic at the three boundaries: 23:59:30 `fresh`→`fresh`; exact 00:00 `fresh`→`stale`; 00:05 `aging`→`stale`. One regression per boundary on each side (`mission-control-common.test.sh` noon/next-midnight-anchored triple; `dashboard-render-smoke.js` same three via a pinned `Date` proxy). Landed as `e380d65`; reinstalled from that SHA (stamp `head`, verify ok, installed `index.html` + `mission_control_common.py` byte-identical to HEAD); full record below.
- 2026-07-11 | ER-109 Morning Brief merge-gate round 6 (side audit `019f4f6c`, governor handshake `50536d40`) — reproduced-then-fixed seven behavioral counterexamples across three claims. (1) Installer honesty/safety (`scripts/dashboard` `do_install`): an asset missing at HEAD fell back to the worktree while stamped `provenance=head`; a deleted-upstream `index.html`/vendor asset lingered installed and got stamped under the new HEAD (the `rm -rf vendor` was coupled to the `index.html` branch, so the absent-both skip-path left both stale); an unwritable destination returned `rc=0` while silently omitting the stamp entry; and a **symlinked destination was followed, writing OUTSIDE the target dir** (safety). All four now fail loud (`rc=1`, no false stamp) via a new `_mc_write` helper (refuses symlink/non-regular-file dests, writes temp+atomic-mv, verifies) plus an `install_failed` gate that refuses to stamp on any failure; head-provenance now fails on a missing committed source instead of a worktree fallback. (2) Validity horizon (`mission_control_common.feed_health` + `index.html` guard): the round-4 flat 48h bound accepted a ~47h-future `valid_until` and let a 30h-old brief read fresh; both now cap at `next_local_midnight(epoch)` on Python and JS. (3) Skew UI (`index.html`): a future `generated_epoch` turned the banner + strip dot amber but left the Home Chats card and the "Open work" strip segment green; both now read `guard()`'s `g.flag` so all four surfaces agree. Landed as `0a4b62f`; reinstalled from that SHA (stamp `head`, 5 files + 2 assets, `verify_install_stamp` ok, installed `index.html` byte-identical to HEAD); one committed regression per counterexample (`dashboard.test.sh` `c24`, render-smoke 47h + skew negatives, common noon-anchored 47h/30h negatives); full record below.

## Work Record Log

### 2026-08-18 — Uniform usage reset countdowns
- Problem: Usage cards hid the reset clock unless `resets_epoch` was present. Empty GLM weekly (0% left) and unused 5-hour (100% left, clock not started) showed no wait time. Signed-out Claude weekly hid a known Wednesday 5pm PT reset because remaining percent was null. Panel clocks were static and used different copy from the Usage tab.
- Reasoning: every window gets the same 12px countdown line. Known epoch counts down even when full. Empty windows prefix `empty —` and turn red. Missing clocks get an honest fallback instead of a blank.
- Diagnosis inputs: live `~/.mission-control/data/headroom.json` (`glm:week` remaining 0 with null epoch; `glm:5h` remaining 100 with null epoch; Claude weekly signed out); z.ai `nextResetTime` in milliseconds; CodexBar reset-countdown pattern and Tabler card scanability.
- Implementation inputs: `dashboard/index.html`, `dashboard/panel.html`, fixtures, dashboard/panel browser tests, render-smoke, companion collector in global-implementations.
- Fix: always attach a countdown; tick every second; unify copy (`resets in` / `renews in` / `empty —` / unused 5-hour / prepaid). Guard `setInterval` so ER-134's interval-free panel sandbox still renders.
- Self-audit:
  - method: `node scripts/dashboard-render-smoke.js .`; `node scripts/dashboard-browser.test.js`; `node scripts/panel-browser.test.js`; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 /bin/bash scripts/er134-usability.test.sh`; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 /bin/bash scripts/verify.sh`.
  - outcome: render-smoke 8 tabs; dashboard browser 323; panel browser 14/0; ER-134 63/0; full verifier `SUITES PASS=26 FAIL=0`.
  - did not verify: live Anthropic 5-hour clock (Keychain empty); inventing a GLM 5-hour reset while unused; menu-bar title countdown.
- Ripple Check: COHERENCE dependency row, Work Record, Completed, Active Branch Ledger, Test Evidence, and `records/2026-08-18-usage-reset-countdowns.md` updated together. Companion collector change lives in global-implementations.
- by: Cursor `46ba944f-41f8-41c8-8d6b-c0b76bce90bb`
- triggered by: Trevor asking every usage surface to say when it resets and count down, especially when empty and waiting.
- led to: `records/2026-08-18-usage-reset-countdowns.md`
- linear: self-contained; repo-only.

### 2026-08-06 — Mission Control sticky exit-1 (chats degraded)
- Problem: `com.gillettes.mission-control` exited 1 on every scheduled run; Automation showed Mission Control red; `dashboard status` reported `chats … fresh (degraded: chat-source)`.
- Reasoning: catch-up correctly skips cosmetic enrichment, so a sticky `metadata_enrichment_status=degraded` is re-exported forever unless an unbounded enrich succeeds. The unbounded path was failing because the default metadata subprocess timeout (20s) is far below live CCI batch cost (~70s for five ids).
- Diagnosis inputs: `launchctl print gui/501/com.gillettes.mission-control`; `~/.mission-control/data/chats.json`; sqlite `meta` on `~/.chat-graph/graph.db`; timed `chat-source metadata --jsonl` batches; `scripts/dashboard` `cmd_collect`; `scripts/chat-graph` `_metadata_timeout_s` / `_enrich_sessions` / `_CATCHUP_INGEST`.
- Implementation inputs: `scripts/chat-graph`, `scripts/dashboard`, `scripts/chat-graph.test.sh`, `scripts/dashboard.test.sh`.
- Fix: raised default `CHAT_GRAPH_METADATA_TIMEOUT_S` to 180; stopped treating chat-source-only `stale_providers` as scheduled `collect --due` failure while keeping status visibility; healed live enrichment to ok (49/49); added cases 19g and scheduled-exit regression.
- Self-audit:
  - method: reproduced sticky degraded meta + timed live metadata batches; ran owning suites and render/browser gates; forced `collect --force chats` then plist-shaped `zsh -lc … collect --due` against installed dashboard with baked `REPO_ROOT`.
  - outcome: suites green; live chats feed `stale []` / `meta ok`; plist-shaped `collect --due` exit 0.
  - did not verify: a natural wall-clock StartInterval fire after commit/install until kickstart/next tick evidence is captured in the same closeout.
- Ripple Check: behavior lives in dashboard/chat-graph + their tests; no AGENTS/PROJECT_INTENT contract change; todo Work Record + Completed updated in this change.
- Confirmed-process repair: failure class was timeout-vs-live-cost mismatch plus scheduled exit treating unhealable cosmetic stale as hard red. Accumulated after catch-up skipped enrichment while a timed-out unbounded enrich left sticky meta. Prevention: timeout floor + scheduled-exit carve-out for chat-source-only stale.
- by: Cursor session (mission-control refresher exit-1 repair).
- triggered by: Trevor ask to fix Mission Control refresher persistent exit-1.

### 2026-08-05 — Scheduled chats catch-up performs zero metadata-store traversals
- Problem: the five-minute `com.gillettes.mission-control` collector runs `dashboard collect --due`, whose chats feed runs `chat-graph export --json --catchup-limit 1500`. A stale export drives a bounded `ingest --limit-files N`, and that ingest ran `_enrich_sessions`, which reaches outside the repo into the provider metadata stores to fetch session titles and repos. On 2026-07-31 that lane was measured at 199.20 MB/s on disk0 and the job was unloaded for containment.
- Reasoning: the titles and repos enrichment fetches are cosmetic — the graph, its edges, and the export are all complete without them. Paying a store-wide traversal for cosmetics on a lane that fires every five minutes is the wrong trade, and it is one this repo cannot make safely because the cost lives in the callee. Skipping the scheduled lane is smaller and more honest than trying to bound a traversal owned by another repo.
- Diagnosis inputs: `scripts/chat-graph` `_enrich_sessions` / `_metadata_batch` / `cmd_ingest` / `cmd_export`; `/Users/gillettes/Library/LaunchAgents/com.gillettes.mission-control.plist`; `diff -q` of `/Users/gillettes/.mission-control/bin/chat-graph` against `scripts/chat-graph`; direct run of `/Users/gillettes/.codex/scripts/chat-source metadata --jsonl <id>`.
- Implementation inputs: `scripts/chat-graph`, `scripts/chat-graph.test.sh`, `scripts/verify.sh`, `DASHBOARD_INSTALL_NO_LAUNCHD=1 scripts/dashboard install`.
- Fix: added module flag `_CATCHUP_INGEST`, set in `cmd_ingest` from `limit is not None` — the same predicate that already decides whether the completion marker is written, reused so the two lanes can never disagree about which run is the scheduled one. `_enrich_sessions` returns immediately when it is set. Unbounded nightly/manual ingest still enriches in one batch, and a deferred title lands on the next unbounded pass. Regression cases 19e and 19f added.
- Self-audit:
  - method: `/bin/bash scripts/chat-graph.test.sh` (ALL PASS) then full `/bin/bash scripts/verify.sh`; RED proof by deleting the two-line gate and re-running, which turned exactly the three new catch-up assertions into failures reporting `calls=1`; install read back with `diff -q` and a `grep` for `_CATCHUP_INGEST` in the installed copy; `launchctl print gui/501/com.gillettes.mission-control` before and after to confirm the job stayed absent.
  - outcome: `SUITES PASS=21 FAIL=0` with `dashboard-browser: 253 assertions passed`; installed runtime byte-identical to the repo source at `0041f16`.
  - did not verify: a natural low-I/O collection cycle, because bootstrapping `com.gillettes.mission-control` was explicitly out of scope for this task — the job remains unloaded and that canary is still owed.
  - surprise finding: the per-session `describe` fan-out named in the 2026-07-31 incident was already replaced by a single batched call in `886efb8`/`9a0320b`/`ee54328`, and that code was already installed. The remaining live gap is that the installed `chat-source` (a symlink into `Coding Projects/Best Coding Workflows`) has no `metadata` subcommand at all — it prints usage and exits 2 — so every scheduled tick spawned a subprocess that could only ever report degraded. That repo was out of scope and was not edited; case 19f pins the behaviour this repo must keep while it stays that way.
- Ripple Check: Completed row, this Work Record, and the Audit and Test Evidence logs updated together. No dashboard, panel, or launchd surface changed; no live data was mutated.
- by: Claude Code (Opus 5), session `bbf68776-9f07-42a4-bd3c-f579177224ea`
- triggered by: the Mission Control chats catch-up I/O repair row in `/Users/gillettes/Coding Projects/global-implementations/todo.md`
- led to: commit `0041f16`; a follow-up note that `chat-source` needs a `metadata` subcommand before enrichment can succeed at all
- linear: self-contained incident follow-up; repo-only.

### 2026-07-31 — Interrupted dashboard fixture reaper
- Problem: an externally interrupted `scripts/dashboard.test.sh` left c39's TERM/INT-ignoring synthetic `chat-graph export` feeder alive under `PPID 1` for about 47 minutes, along with temp roots outside the suite's only `EXIT` cleanup root.
- Reasoning: each synthetic process must remain attributable to one suite instance after its direct parent disappears. PID-only or name-based cleanup could kill a reused or unrelated process, so the outer harness needs a suite-private process registry plus a birth-identity check and must discover descendant PID files only when their current command is rooted inside the same unique fixture tree.
- Diagnosis inputs: orphan PID 54749 command/cwd/PPID evidence from ER-277 terminal cleanup; exact byte match to c39's feeder fixture; the pre-fix outer `trap 'rm -rf "$ROOT"' EXIT`; all background spawn and `mktemp -d` call sites in `scripts/dashboard.test.sh`.
- Implementation inputs: `scripts/dashboard.test.sh` only; macOS `/bin/ps`, `/usr/bin/find`, `/usr/bin/mktemp`, and Bash 3.2-compatible traps/functions.
- Fix: replaced the delete-only EXIT trap with idempotent EXIT/INT/TERM/HUP cleanup; records every direct synthetic child as exact PID plus launch timestamp, admits descendant PID files only when the live command is rooted below the suite's unique temp root, sends bounded TERM then exact-identity KILL, and confines anonymous fixture temp files/directories beneath that root. Added c39a, which interrupts a nested outer harness around a stubborn synthetic descendant and proves both process and root are gone.
- Self-audit:
  - method: observed the new c39a fail before implementation because no interruption probe became ready; reran it green after the guard; inspected every background spawn/PID-file/temp-root call site; ran Bash syntax, whitespace, exact survivor checks, and live-service negative readback. Reviewed the diff for PID reuse, broad-name kills, signal recursion, unbounded waits, and real-home access.
  - outcome: c39a and shell/static boundaries pass (`PASS=7 FAIL=0`); no task-owned dashboard/test process remains. The complete dashboard suite was attempted once with Apple `/bin/bash`, then interrupted after the degraded host made unrelated c0/c1 collectors exceed their existing fixture deadlines; the exact suite/collector PIDs and an already-completed worktree hook group were retired and verified absent.
  - did not verify: a clean full dashboard matrix on this boot because persistent `syspolicyd` crashes were concurrently delaying direct script execution; no installed runtime, live collection, LaunchAgent execution, or dashboard state was exercised.
- Ripple Check: this is test-harness-only; `scripts/dashboard`, installed assets, schemas, launchd files, README, and product behavior do not depend on it. The existing dashboard release gate automatically includes c39a on the next healthy full run.
- Confirmed-process repair: failure class was child lifecycle ownership ending at the normal case cleanup rather than at the outer test-runner boundary. It accumulated when an external interruption bypassed c39's local post-wait kill and the only outer trap deleted a different root. Earlier detection is the c39a signal regression plus post-suite exact process/root checks. Bounded analogous review covered every background spawn, PID-file fixture, and anonymous `mktemp` call in this owning suite; no other repo or live runtime was scanned.

### 2026-07-31 — ER-277 Packet D collector backoff and batch metadata
- Problem: Chats enrichment ran up to 50 sequential `chat-source describe` subprocesses; timeout rolled back metadata, left last-good stale, and immediately made the five-minute collection due again. Feed errors lacked persistent attempt/backoff truth.
- Reasoning: one bounded metadata batch process group removes repeated scans, while feed-local persistent backoff prevents a failed producer from grinding without blocking healthy siblings. Core graph export must stay available even when metadata enrichment is absent or partial.
- Diagnosis inputs: accepted ER-277 design Packet D; `scripts/chat-graph`, `scripts/dashboard`, their focused tests, existing feed error sidecars, and source history.
- Implementation inputs: `scripts/chat-graph`, `scripts/dashboard`, their focused tests, public Mission Control docs, and durable records.
- Fix: replaced up to 50 sequential describe calls with one ordered metadata batch subprocess group; TERM/KILL cleanup reaps non-cooperative descendants; updates and persistent health stay in the ingest transaction; unresolved attempts retain the weekly eligibility window, while successful internal-fallback rows resolve in the same ingest instead of being treated as CCI misses. ### 2026-07-24 — ER-156 producer migration to purpose routes
- Problem: decisions and Morning Brief delivery selected raw destination identifiers in
  Mission Control, and the deadman reused the authorization identity as a destination.
  Those contracts could bypass the four-route policy or send an incident to the wrong surface.
- Reasoning: destination policy belongs in mobile-connect. Producers should emit a fixed
  route/event contract, while the independent deadman may read only the dedicated Incidents
  route key because it deliberately does not depend on the primary sender.
- Diagnosis inputs: `/tmp/telegram-mission-control-red.txt` records the expected pre-fix
  failures for route-aware decision cards, fixed Briefs event argv, Incidents-only deadman
  configuration, and rejection of the authorization identity as a destination.
- Implementation inputs: `scripts/decision-alert`, `scripts/morning-brief`,
  `scripts/morning-brief-deadman`, their focused tests, and the existing ER-156 route design.
- Fix: decision alerts invoke `decision-send --route control`; normal Morning Brief chunks
  invoke `emit --route briefs` with a fixed source/event/severity/batch/part contract and
  confirm only `delivered` or `deduplicated`; the deadman reads
  only `MORNING_BRIEF_INCIDENTS_CHAT_ID` or `MC_ROUTE_INCIDENTS_CHAT_ID`; raw destination
  fallback was removed from all three production paths. Provider failure now propagates from
  mobile-connect, so decision-alert cannot stamp a false receipt.
- Self-audit:
  - method: focused decision, brief, delivery, deadman, dashboard, and ER-134 suites;
    the complete offline verifier; source scans for forbidden destination fallback; diff review.
  - outcome: focused suites pass, including the new suppression-status and dashboard
    diagnostic regressions; `/tmp/er156-producer-review-mission-full-green.txt` records
    the complete verifier at `21/21`. The real cross-repo adapter proof confirms failed
    decisions remain retryable and six brief parts deliver exactly once.
  - did not verify: no canonical merge, install, provider contact, or live route canary was
    performed; those remain gated on mobile-connect landing and the private route bindings.
- Ripple Check: updated producer docs, call-shape tests, deadman configuration tests,
  Active Next Steps, branch state, Test Evidence, and Feedback Decision records together.
- by: Codex `019f674e-a794-71f0-a6dc-ca861fec4864`

### 2026-07-11 — Chat-graph full-ingest health truth repair
- Problem: A fresh governor check reported `chat-graph doctor` failed because the full-ingest marker was 134 minutes old, even though full transcript ingestion is a nightly 23:30 job with the already-established 30-hour SLA and the graph database/feed were being refreshed incrementally. `show` repeated the same false warning after 30 minutes, while a completely missing marker incorrectly passed doctor.
- Decision: Keep the 30-minute constant only for export's bounded incremental catch-up. `show` and `doctor` describe the completed full-ingest marker, so they must use the shared `full_ingest_sla_s()` policy already consumed by dashboard and brief health. Missing full-ingest evidence must fail closed.
- Implementation: `scripts/chat-graph` now labels `STALE_INGEST_S` as catch-up-only; `show` warns when the full ingest is absent, future-dated, or beyond the shared SLA; `doctor` requires `0 <= age <= SLA` and prints the evaluated SLA beside the age. `scripts/chat-graph.test.sh` clears ambient SLA overrides, time-travels the marker across healthy 3-hour, stale 31-hour, future 1-hour, and missing boundaries, and proves explicit 1-hour/48-hour overrides.
- Red-before-green evidence: Before `245fb31`, the new suite had exactly three failures: doctor rejected the healthy 3-hour marker, show warned on it, and doctor accepted a missing marker. An independent immutable review then reproduced a future-marker false green; its +1-hour regression failed in exactly two places before `dcf11c2` (doctor accepted it and show omitted a warning). The genuinely stale 31-hour controls already failed/warned and remained so.
- Verification: `python3 -m py_compile scripts/chat-graph`, the complete `scripts/chat-graph.test.sh` suite (`ALL PASS`), and `git diff --check` pass through `9843607`. Independent review of immutable range `245fb317^..9843607` was `REVIEW-CLEAN` with no P0-P3 findings; the same full suite also passed under ambient SLA `3600`, and `mission-control-common.test.sh` passed `2/2`. Exact committed head `5372dfa` was installed with a verified schema-1 five-runtime/two-asset stamp; the installed dashboard remains baked to the canonical repo, whose reviewed `chat-graph` hash is `3768afd6...`. Live doctor is green at age 146 minutes under the 1800-minute SLA, Chats refresh succeeds, every feed is fresh, and the Mission Control LaunchAgent advanced runs 86→87 with exit 0. Overall dashboard status remains exit 1 only because Automation truthfully contains an unrelated red job.
- Self-audit: The 30-minute export catch-up behavior at line 2284 remains unchanged; only human health/readout consumers moved to the nightly policy. ### 2026-07-11 — Chat truth-layer timeout and cache trust repair
- Problem: A live dashboard collection ran the expensive portfolio Git scan and then `chat-graph export` ran it again for repository annotations. Under concurrent load the second scan exceeded the Chats timeout, the dashboard killed only the direct parent, the scanner survived, and an empty `ingest.lock` remained. The Git and Chats feeds became error/stale. The first cache-first repair passed canonical suites but an independent reviewer proved malformed cache rows could crash or falsely resolve open work, alternate Mission Control homes and explicit scanner overrides were ignored, a failed Git cycle still launched the scan again, and timeout descendants still survived.
- Decision: Treat this as a truth-layer repair, not an executor feature. Reuse only a strict current Git envelope; reject the entire cache on any malformed row; preserve standalone/explicit scanner behavior; represent same-cycle upstream failure as explicit uncertainty without reconciling dirty work; and terminate/reap the complete feeder process group while allowing `chat-graph` to unwind its lock.
- Implementation: `chat-graph` now resolves the cache from `CHAT_GRAPH_GIT_FEED` or the active `MISSION_CONTROL_HOME`, requires exact schema/feed/cadence/epoch/boolean/integer/list contracts plus unique valid repository rows from both cache and scanner, and keeps an explicit scanner authoritative unless both paths are supplied. The same-cycle skip signal is checked before cache or scanner access, and unavailable/invalid evidence never reconciles open repository truth. Each ingest lock carries PID, per-run nonce, and `/bin/ps` process-start identity; age permits recovery only when that exact identity is absent or changed, while probe failure preserves the lock. Normal release still requires the complete owner record. Dashboard passes its exact Git feed and nonce to built-in Chats, carries a same-cycle Git error/lock signal, and runs every feeder in a new session with bounded TERM/grace/SIGKILL/pipe-close/reap cleanup. Cleanup authorization requires an unreaped owned leader plus a proven final group signal/absence; timeout and interruption cleanup remove only the killed feeder's matching lock. Invalid UTF-8 is replacement-decoded so it fails at the payload boundary instead of raising after leader reap. The export remains shape-compatible with `repo_annotations=[]` plus a static uncertainty note, and runtime imports do not write bytecode beside source.
- Red-before-green evidence: The original cache probes failed on forged envelopes, wrong state home, implicit-cache precedence, duplicate scanning, false reconciliation, descendant survival, and orphan locks. Independent re-review then reproduced four additional graph failures (fresh last-good cache defeating the current-cycle skip, missing owner metadata, malformed fallback mutation/crash, and duplicate fallback trust) plus dashboard failures for a TERM-cooperative leader with a TERM-resistant child, an escaped descriptor holder, and interrupted-engine lock cleanup. Final challenge reproduced two more P1s: a 31-minute-old lock with an exact live PID was stolen, and invalid UTF-8 raised only after leader reap so a same-group child survived while its lock was falsely deleted. Exact live-start/PID-reuse and post-reap invalid-byte regressions are now GREEN.
- Verification: `chat-graph.test.sh` `ALL PASS`; dashboard `PASS=55 FAIL=0`; Python in-memory compile, Bash syntax, source-bytecode scan, and `git diff --check` pass. The strict fallback contract accepts the real 58-repository scanner payload. Implementation commits `e2f5d60` + `4de0aef` and closeout `9191ebe` are on `origin/main`; canonical `/Users/gillettes/Coding Projects/mission-control` is at the closeout tree, while the installed stamp verifies exact implementation head `4de0aef` with the dashboard baked to that durable canonical repo path. Live strict Automation+Git+Chats collection produced three fresh `ok:true` feeds, 58 Git rows, 58 chat annotations, no uncertainty notes, no skipped ingest, no surviving scanner/export process, and no ingest-lock residue. A real `com.gillettes.mission-control` kickstart advanced runs 70→71 and exited 0.
- Self-audit: Cached facts are reused only inside the successful collection cycle; a failed cycle leaves prior dirty items unresolved. A descendant that deliberately creates a different session can outlive the feeder group, but inherited pipe descriptors are closed so it cannot wedge collection; because the old group is no longer provable after leader reap, its lock remains fail-closed for owner-aware stale recovery rather than being falsely deleted. The first-party feeder does not escape its session. Four historical transient scan warnings remain advisory. Scanner runtime measured 35.87 seconds without contention, so no timeout increase, process-tree crawler, or new parallel scanner was added.
- Review: Independent review and re-review findings are represented by executable regressions, including the final live-owner and post-reap P1s. Reviewer `/root/external_orchestration_research` returned `READY` / review-clean with no P0-P3 findings for frozen five-path diff SHA-256 `7b75fe6953657a32fc2ead772a2d88fd4f11d532741c70a1bae61d928fea9148`; canonical containment, exact install verification, and live-current proof are now complete.
- Rollback: Revert the task commit. The scanner-only path returns, but duplicate-scan/timeout risk returns too; remove only a proven empty orphan lock after confirming no owner process.
- Source: Codex thread `019f51c1-5817-7872-a6ce-8b65428277ed`; independent reviewer `/root/external_orchestration_research`; linear: repo-only.

### 2026-07-11 — Morning Brief merge-gate round 7: valid_until is a hard expiry across the exact-midnight rollover (ER-109)
- Problem: The parent Fable governor (handshake `5cf8866d-818c-405b-a43b-0c40193fc971`, chat `e85411a1`) confirmed a final merge-blocker on the post-round-6 tree (`0a4b62f`): `feed_health({generated_epoch: 23:59, valid_until: next-midnight}, cadence=300, now=exact-midnight)` returned `state=fresh red=False`, and at 00:05 returned `state=aging` — an EXPIRED daily brief was reading fresh/aging (both non-red) instead of stale. Root cause: `valid_until` was implemented ONLY as a "stay fresh until" window (`elif valid_ok and now < valid_until: fresh`). At `now == valid_until` that check is false (strict `<`), so the verdict fell THROUGH to the poll-cadence age ladder — a 60s-old brief (age < cadence) landed in the `else: fresh` bucket at midnight, and a 360s-old brief (age > cadence) landed in `aging` at 00:05. Nothing treated passing `valid_until` as expiry.
- Reasoning: The correct semantics per the governor is that a present `valid_until` is a HARD expiry, not just a fresh-extension: once `now` reaches it, the daily product is over regardless of how young the compose epoch is on the poll cadence. Implemented as a single new branch on BOTH sides, placed AFTER skew and BEFORE the fresh-window branch: `valid_until present and now >= valid_until ⇒ state=stale, red=True`. Placement matters — it must precede the fresh-window and age-ladder branches so expiry wins. It is additive to the round-6 horizon logic (`valid_ok = valid_until <= next_local_midnight(epoch)`): the horizon check still rejects malformed far-future `valid_until` from EXTENDING freshness; the new branch adds that a legitimate `valid_until`, once reached, ENDS freshness. The existing far-future/47h/30h negatives all have `now < valid_until`, so the new branch never fires for them and they fall through exactly as before (re-verified green).
- Diagnosis inputs: parent Fable governor handshake `5cf8866d-818c-405b-a43b-0c40193fc971` (chat `e85411a1`, title "ER-109 benefit pilot governor"); `scripts/mission_control_common.py` `feed_health` (the `valid_ok`/fresh-window/age-ladder chain, ~lines 392-408); `dashboard/index.html` `guard` (the JS `validOk`/flag chain, ~lines 567-575, page `nowS()` = `Date.now()/1000`). Reproduced synthetically before fixing (old-logic simulation vs `feed_health`) at all three boundary clocks.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`. Code — `scripts/mission_control_common.py`: added `elif valid_until is not None and now >= valid_until: state=stale, red=True` before the fresh-window branch. `dashboard/index.html`: added `else if (validUntil && nowS() >= validUntil) flag = "stale";` before the fresh-window branch (mirrors the Python). Tests — `mission-control-common.test.sh`: a noon→`next_local_midnight`-anchored triple (compose `MID-60` = 23:59 local, `valid_until = MID`): `fresh` at `MID-30`, `stale`/red at exact `MID`, `stale`/red at `MID+300`. `dashboard-render-smoke.js`: the same three boundaries via a `Proxy(Date)` that pins `Date.now()` to `(MID+offset)*1000` while passing `new Date(...)` through, asserting the "Data is older than expected" banner is absent at 23:59:30 and shown at exact-midnight and 00:05.
- Test flips: none. No existing assertion was changed or reversed; the round-6 horizon negatives (far-future, 47h, 30h, 3-day, within-horizon fresh) all still pass unchanged. Only additive regressions.
- Before/after synthetic (composed 23:59, `valid_until` = next local midnight, cadence 300):
  - `23:59:30`  BEFORE `state=fresh red=False`  → AFTER `state=fresh red=False`
  - `00:00:00 (exact midnight)`  BEFORE `state=fresh red=False`  → AFTER `state=stale red=True`
  - `00:05:00`  BEFORE `state=aging red=False`  → AFTER `state=stale red=True`
- Fix: landed as commit `e380d65`, pushed `feature/morning-brief-activation`; reinstalled the live `~/.mission-control` runtime + assets from that SHA.
- Test Evidence / Self-audit:
  - method: cleared `scripts/__pycache__` and cold-ran every required suite after the edits.

### 2026-07-11 — Morning Brief merge-gate round 6: installer honesty/safety + next-midnight validity + skew UI consistency (ER-109, side-audit re-verify)
- Problem: A side audit (Codex thread `019f4f6c`, pinned to pre-round-5 `ecb05f7`) claimed three defect classes; the parent Fable governor (handshake `50536d40`) re-verified the code shape as plausible and asked for a behavioral counterexample per sub-claim BEFORE fixing. Seven counterexamples were built as tests and run RED against HEAD first; all seven reproduced (1(b) was NOT cleanly refuted — a coupled skip-path reproduced it live).
  - (1) **Installer honesty/safety** (`scripts/dashboard` `do_install`, ~lines 547-640). (a) When `provenance=head` (a git repo) but a shipped asset was absent at HEAD yet present in the worktree, the `index.html` install and the main `dashboard` runtime fell back to the worktree copy while the stamp still recorded `provenance=head` — a provenance lie. (b) When `index.html` was absent at BOTH HEAD and the worktree, neither install branch ran, so the pre-existing `rm -rf vendor` (round-5 `b47e14f`, which lives INSIDE the index.html branches) never fired: a stale installed `index.html` and stale `vendor/*` lingered and were re-stamped under the new `head_sha`. (c) A blocked/unwritable destination (e.g. a directory at the dest path) let `git show … > "$dest"` fail while bash (no `set -e`) continued to `rc=0`; `write_install_stamp` only hashes files that `os.path.isfile`, so the failed file was silently omitted from the stamp. (d) **Safety:** a symlinked destination was followed — `git show … > "$dest"` and `cp` write THROUGH the link — so the installer wrote OUTSIDE the target dir (the red run clobbered an outside file with the `morning-brief` runtime body).
  - (2) **Validity semantics** (`mission_control_common.feed_health` + the `index.html` per-feed `guard`). Round-4 bounded a daily product's `valid_until` with a flat `_MAX_VALID_HORIZON_S = 2*86400` (48h). But a daily product's true horizon is the next local midnight (<=~24-25h). Governor synthetics reproduced: a ~47h-future `valid_until` on an old brief read `fresh`, and a 30h-old brief carrying a 47h-span `valid_until` read `fresh` — a stale brief suppressing staleness for up to two days. Commit `b949948` ("exact validity horizon") only tested the same-day migrate path and the 3-day rejection; it did NOT close the 24-48h band.
  - (3) **Skew UI consistency** (`index.html`). A future `generated_epoch` (`feed_health`/guard state `skew`) turned the amber banner (`panel`) and the strip freshness dot (already `g.flag === "skew"` aware) amber, but `cardChats` (~line 1298) and `feedSummary` (the "Open work" strip segment, ~line 1064) computed their state without the skew flag, so the Home Chats card AND the Open work segment stayed green while the banner/dot warned — the four surfaces disagreed.
- Reasoning: Fixes must fail loud and never over-claim. (1) A single `_mc_write` helper enforces the write invariants in one place: refuse a symlink dest (never write through a link — solves (d)); refuse a non-regular-file dest (dir/special); write to a temp in the same dir then atomic `mv` (a blocked write can't leave a half-file or silent no-op — solves (c)); return nonzero on any failure. Callers set an `install_failed` flag via `|| install_failed=1` in the PARENT shell (the pipe RHS runs in a subshell, so the flag is set by the caller, not inside `_mc_write`). Under `provenance=head`, a source missing at HEAD now fails loud instead of a worktree fallback (solves (a)); the `index.html`-absent-both path drops the stale shell+vendor and fails loud (solves (b)). A final gate returns `rc=1` and writes NO stamp when `install_failed`, so a copy that never landed is never stamped as a clean committed surface. `provenance=worktree` (a non-git fixture) stays legitimate and unchanged — only the head-path lies were closed. (2) The correct horizon IS `next_local_midnight(epoch)` (DST-correct via `mktime`/`new Date(y,mon,day+1)`); a compose-stamped `valid_until` equals that cap (`<=` inclusive), while 47h and 30h-old both exceed it and fall to the age ladder → stale. The governor's own handoff states the intent is next-local-midnight, so this tightens round-4, it is not a unilateral reversal. (3) Skew is `now < generated_epoch` — inherently render-time, so the producer cannot pre-export it the way round-5 exported `full_ingest_stale`; `guard()`'s `g.flag` IS the single-source analog, and `cardChats`/`feedSummary` now read it (the banner and dot already did), honoring the round-5 pattern's spirit.
- Diagnosis inputs: parent Fable governor handshake `50536d40-d6f9-47af-b96e-e673461f0ec1` (chat `e85411a1`); side audit `019f4f6c`; `scripts/dashboard` `do_install`; `scripts/mission_control_common.py` (`feed_health`, `next_local_midnight`, `write_install_stamp`/`verify_install_stamp`); `dashboard/index.html` (`guard`, `panel`, `feedSummary`, `renderStrip` dots, `cardChats`); the round-5/round-4 tests (`dashboard.test.sh` `c21`, `mission-control-common.test.sh`, `dashboard-render-smoke.js`). Reproduction was test-first: 4 installer counterexamples (temp git repos) + 2 Python validity synthetics + 2 JS skew-surface class assertions, all RED against HEAD `396ab49` before any fix.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`. Code — `scripts/dashboard`: added `_mc_write` + `install_failed` gate; routed the main dashboard runtime, the runtime loop, and the index.html/vendor installs through it; head-missing sources fail loud. `scripts/mission_control_common.py`: `feed_health` `valid_ok` now `valid_until <= next_local_midnight(epoch)`; removed the dead `_MAX_VALID_HORIZON_S`. `dashboard/index.html`: added a JS `nextLocalMidnight(sec)` helper and swapped the guard's flat-48h check for it; `cardChats` and `feedSummary` now go amber on `g.flag === "skew"` (card also shows a "clock skew" line). Tests — `dashboard.test.sh` new `c24` (four installer counterexamples + clean-install sanity, registered in the run list, PASS count 36→37); `dashboard-render-smoke.js` two new negatives (a 40h-old brief with a 47h `valid_until` shows the stale banner; a future chats `generated_epoch` turns the Open work segment glyph AND the Chats card headline `mc-state-amber`) plus a DST-safe rewrite of the base brief fixture (compose at local-midnight-today, valid to next local midnight) so the tighter cap is not midnight-flaky; `mission-control-common.test.sh` a noon-anchored within-horizon assertion + the exact 47h and 30h-old negatives.
- Test flips: the round-4 `mission-control-common.test.sh` "within-horizon honored" case was noon-anchored (was `NOW`-relative, which the tighter next-midnight cap would make time-of-day flaky) — a determinism realignment that keeps the same assertion, not a weakening; its neighbor 3-day-rejection and 2001/2100-far-future assertions are unchanged and still pass. The `dashboard-render-smoke.js` base brief fixture validity window was made midnight-safe for the same reason. No assertion was reversed to pass.
- Fix: landed as commit `0a4b62f`, pushed `feature/morning-brief-activation`; reinstalled the live `~/.mission-control` runtime + assets from that SHA.
- Test Evidence / Self-audit:

  - outcome: `mission-control-common` PASS=2 FAIL=0; `morning-brief` ALL PASS; `morning-brief-delivery` ALL PASS; `morning-brief-deadman` ALL PASS; `dashboard --require-shell` PASS=37 FAIL=0 (36→37 for `c24`); `dashboard-render-smoke` all 7 tabs + the far-future, 47h-band, skew-consistency, and both full-ingest assertions PASS; `chat-graph` ALL PASS (run because it imports the changed `mission_control_common`).

The activation-gated jobs (`morning-brief`, `morning-brief-deadman`, `outcome-extractor`) self-skipped and were left untouched.

### 2026-07-11 — Morning Brief merge-gate round 5 P2: single-source full-ingest freshness flag (ER-109, Codex re-audit)
- Problem: Codex re-audit thread `019f5010-185b-79f2-8767-4f9a5f457e64` found `dashboard/index.html` still classified the chats full-ingest signal as stale using a hardcoded `"last_full_ingest_age_s" in cnt && (cnt.last_full_ingest_age_s == null || +cnt.last_full_ingest_age_s > 1800)` comparison, duplicated in three places (`feedSummary` ~line 1062, the strip freshness dots ~line 1112-1115, `cardChats` ~line 1293), while the CLI/brief side (`mission_control_common.nested_ingest_stale`, wired in round 4/`ecb05f7`) already uses the truthful 30h nightly SLA. Effect: a healthy last-night ingest (well inside the 30h SLA) showed "full chat scan is stale" on the Home card and status strip ~30 minutes after compose, contradicting `dashboard status`'s correct `fresh` read for the same feed — the same policy encoded twice, drifted.
- Reasoning: The correct fix is a single source of policy, not a second JS re-implementation of the SLA math. `scripts/chat-graph` (the chats feed producer) already imports from `mission_control_common` and already computes `last_full_ingest_age_s`/`ingest_skipped` at export time; it now also calls the EXISTING `nested_ingest_stale()` (unchanged — no Python policy logic touched) against its own freshly-built `counts` dict and stamps the boolean result as `counts.full_ingest_stale`. `index.html`'s three sites now read that one computed flag instead of re-deriving a threshold, so there is exactly one place (`nested_ingest_stale`) that decides "is the full ingest stale," consumed identically by `dashboard status`, the morning brief, and the JS renderer.
- Diagnosis inputs: parent Fable governor handshake `ea6d00bb-dfb0-4b61-9326-bde69bcbcff9` (chat `e85411a1`); Codex re-audit thread `019f5010-185b-79f2-8767-4f9a5f457e64`; `dashboard/index.html` (`feedSummary`, `renderStrip`, `cardChats`); `scripts/mission_control_common.py` (`nested_ingest_stale`, unchanged); `scripts/chat-graph` (`cmd_export`/counts block ~line 2152-2166).
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`. Code: `scripts/chat-graph` imports `nested_ingest_stale` from `mission_control_common` and adds `counts["full_ingest_stale"] = nested_ingest_stale({"data": {"counts": counts}})` immediately after `counts` is built (before the envelope `return`), so every chats export carries the flag. `dashboard/index.html`'s three sites replace the hardcoded `> 1800` comparison with `cnt.full_ingest_stale === true` (and the strip-dots site drops the now-redundant inline `"last_full_ingest_age_s" in ...` check); `ingest_skipped` stays a separate read since it drives its own distinct "chat refresh skipped" message line. Tests: `scripts/dashboard-render-smoke.js` gains two new assertions on the Home tab — a fixture with `last_full_ingest_age_s: 3286` + `full_ingest_stale: false` (a healthy nightly ingest: past the OLD buggy 30-minute threshold, comfortably inside the 30h SLA) must NOT render "full chat scan is stale"; a fixture with `last_full_ingest_age_s: 111600` (~31h) + `full_ingest_stale: true` (a genuinely missed nightly) MUST render it. Both fixtures carry the raw age alongside the flag so a regression back to reading the raw age directly would fail the healthy case.
- No test flips: this is additive coverage (two new render-smoke assertions); no existing assertion was weakened or reversed.
- Fix: landed as commit `a32f9cf`, pushed `feature/morning-brief-activation`; reinstalled the live runtime `bin/` + assets from that SHA with the install-stamp updated.
- Test Evidence / Self-audit:
  - method: cold-reran the four required suites after edits (fresh `__pycache__` cleared): `mission-control-common.test.sh`, `morning-brief.test.sh`, `dashboard.test.sh --require-shell`, `dashboard-render-smoke.js`. Additionally cold-ran `chat-graph.test.sh` (not in the required list, but the producer file was edited directly) to confirm no regression there.
  - outcome: `mission-control-common` PASS=2 FAIL=0; `morning-brief` ALL PASS; `dashboard --require-shell` PASS=36 FAIL=0 (36, not round-4's 34, because this branch already carried two additional pre-existing-uncommitted `c23` brief-migrate assertions found and committed separately this session — see the two WIP-preservation commits below); `chat-graph.test.sh` ALL PASS; render-smoke: all 7 tabs render + `PASS: absurd far-future valid_until is rejected` (round-4 regression, unaffected) + `PASS: healthy nightly full ingest (age 3286s) renders NOT-stale` + `PASS: genuinely missed nightly full ingest (age ~31h) renders stale` — all cold.
  - install-stamp: reinstalled via `dashboard install` from `a32f9cf`; `install-stamp.json` provenance `head`, head_sha `a32f9cf6e45ef4418e79e08d340493296f59f901`, 5 `bin/` files + 2 assets; `dashboard status` install row `install a32f9cf6e45e ok verified (head)`. Installed `index.html` sha256 `c8f4d277ae90ff790c045316cf3caca5c261c0e947263ca900b0480b0303c461` MATCHES `git show HEAD:dashboard/index.html | shasum -a 256`, byte for byte. Activation-gated jobs (`morning-brief`, `morning-brief-deadman`, `outcome-extractor`) self-skipped and were left untouched.
  - honest caveat: did not verify live production behavior against the real nightly ingest marker (no live re-run of the actual 23:30 job this session); coverage is via the render-smoke fixtures plus the pre-existing, unchanged `nested_ingest_stale` Python test coverage from round 4 (`mission-control-common.test.sh`, `morning-brief.test.sh`). Did not touch delivery/deadman logic or merge to main, per constraint.

### 2026-07-11 — Morning Brief merge-gate round 4: skew-never-fresh + bounded validity, truthful nightly ingest SLA, install-asset stamp (ER-109, governor-verified)
- Problem: Three independently-verified trust defects (side-audit claims re-verified by the governor against code and live synthetics) survived on `feature/morning-brief-activation`.
  - (1a) **Skew read as fresh.** `scripts/morning-brief` `_input_health` mapped `feed_health` state `skew` (a FUTURE `generated_epoch`, `red=false`) into `fresh` whenever nested ingest was current. Governor synthetic: `feed_health({generated_epoch: now+600}, cadence=300)` → `state=skew, red=false` → brief rendered the input `fresh`. A future/invalid envelope timestamp is never trustworthy.
  - (1b) **Bounded-horizon validity (same class).** `feed_health` (and the `index.html` per-feed guard) honored ANY `valid_until > now`, so a malformed far-future value suppressed staleness forever. The JS render-smoke test literally locked this: `generated_epoch` year 2001 + `valid_until` year 2100 asserted NOT-stale.
  - (2) **Untruthful full-ingest deadline.** `scripts/morning-brief` declares `chats` `cadence_s=1800`, and `nested_ingest_stale` compared `last_full_ingest_age_s` to that 1800s envelope cadence — but the unbounded full transcript ingest runs NIGHTLY, so a HEALTHY last-night ingest reads stale for all but 30 min/day. Live evidence: `~/.chat-graph/last-ingest` mtime `2026-07-10 23:31:21`.
  - (3) **Install-provenance incompleteness.** `install-stamp.json` stamped only the five `bin/` runtimes, while `dashboard install` also deploys `index.html` (which carries the render JS) + `vendor/*`. Those assets were unverifiable, and worse, they were copied from the WORKING TREE while the bin files come from committed HEAD — so extending the stamp naively would have stamped a dirty tree as `head` (a new provenance lie; caught by pre-edit advisor consult).
- Reasoning:
  - (1a) Skew is surfaced as its own distinct visible state, not folded into `stale` — the task requires it visible AND distinct. `_needs_you` already treats any non-`fresh` required input as not-current, and `_machinery` prints `chats input is skew (-600s old)`, so the negative age exposes the future timestamp.
  - (1b) A daily product's validity is a bounded horizon (next local midnight, ≤~24h past compose epoch). `valid_until` is honored only within `_MAX_VALID_HORIZON_S = 2*86400` of the feed's own `generated_epoch` (DST/late-compose slack) — else it falls through to the age ladder. Same constant mirrored in `index.html` so status, the shell guard, and the brief agree.
  - (2) The full-ingest staleness horizon is a NIGHTLY one, not the envelope cadence. Evidence chain, cited: `~/.chat-graph/last-ingest` marker mtime `2026-07-10 23:31:21` → written by `~/.claude/nightly-review/bin/nightly-review.sh:167` (`timeout 600 "$cg" ingest`, a full pass that writes the marker) → scheduled by `com.gillettes.nightly-review` (`StartCalendarInterval` Hour=23 Minute=30 daily). SLA band: a healthy last-night ingest tops out near the 24h cycle (marker stamped at completion, no meaningful export lag); a genuinely missed nightly is ≥~31.5h at the 07:00 brief (~48h general). `_full_ingest_sla_s()` defaults to 30h (`MISSION_CONTROL_FULL_INGEST_SLA_S`), separating the two; an envelope may override with its own completion SLA via `counts.full_ingest_sla_s`.
  - (3) Extend the stamp to the exact installed code/render surface (`assets` map keyed by home-relative path), AND fix the provenance root: assets now install from the SAME committed commit as the runtimes (pinned `git show <sha>:...` reads), with explicit test-only worktree fallback never verifying green. `verify_install_stamp` resolves assets under `os.path.dirname(bin_dir)`, so `status` and deadman cover code/render drift; LaunchAgents use a separate checked deployment/reload gate.
- Diagnosis inputs: parent Fable governor handshake `d174616e-4dd0-4039-a807-ea6201458193` (chat `e85411a1`); `scripts/mission_control_common.py` (`feed_health`, `nested_ingest_stale`, `next_local_midnight`, `write_install_stamp`/`verify_install_stamp`); `scripts/morning-brief` `_input_health`; `scripts/dashboard` (`cmd_status`, `_chats_stale_ingest`, `do_install`); `dashboard/index.html` `guard()`; `scripts/chat-graph` (`_last_full_ingest_epoch`, `cmd_ingest` marker write); launchd `com.gillettes.nightly-review` + `~/.claude/nightly-review/bin/nightly-review.sh`; live marker `~/.chat-graph/last-ingest`; a pre-edit advisor consult that blocked the naive asset-stamp provenance lie.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`. Code: `mission_control_common.py` adds `_MAX_VALID_HORIZON_S`, the bounded `valid_ok` gate in `feed_health`, `_full_ingest_sla_s()` + rewritten `nested_ingest_stale(env)` (dropped the vestigial `cadence` arg — sole caller was `feed_health`), and `assets` support in `write_install_stamp`/`verify_install_stamp`. `morning-brief` `_input_health` maps `skew` to its own state. `index.html` guard adds the matching 2-day horizon clause. `scripts/dashboard` `do_install` installs assets from HEAD before stamping and passes `MC_HOME` + the asset map. Tests: `mission-control-common.test.sh` gains a direct `feed_health`/`nested_ingest_stale`/install-asset block (skew, far-future→stale, within-horizon→fresh, SLA band 7h/26h not-flag + 50h flag, `full_ingest_sla_s` + env override, asset drift/missing). `morning-brief.test.sh` rewrites the nested test two-sided (26h healthy not-flagged, 50h missed flagged) and adds a skew regression. `dashboard-render-smoke.js` main-loop brief becomes a legit within-horizon not-stale case and a post-loop negative render asserts the absurd far-future brief SHOWS stale. `dashboard.test.sh` `c21` adds committed `index.html`+`vendor` and asserts asset stamping + asset drift; `c20` made clock-robust (local-noon anchor + `MISSION_CONTROL_NOW_EPOCH`).
- Authorized test flips (NOT silent weakening — both are the named defects): `morning-brief.test.sh` nested case `26h → stale` (round-3, commit `ed95cce`) → `26h healthy NOT flagged` + new `50h missed flagged`; `dashboard-render-smoke.js` far-future `valid_until` asserted NOT-stale (round-2, commit `069626b`'s locked behavior) → asserted STALE, with a separate legit within-horizon case preserving the honored-validity coverage.

### 2026-07-10 — Morning Brief P1: migrate valid_until on same-day delivered legacy brief (Codex re-audit 019fd4d4)
- Problem: The Codex merge-gate re-audit (thread `019fd4d4-1f40-7a71-b52d-163057d646a5`) found one P1: a same-day ALREADY-DELIVERED brief composed before the `valid_until` field existed is never migrated. `scripts/dashboard` (`_wrap`, ~line 141) honors `valid_until` only when the sidecar already carries it, and `scripts/morning-brief` `_compose_locked` (~line 838) defers recomposing a same-day delivered brief and returned without touching the sidecar. The dashboard's 5-minute poll (`_run_builtin_brief`) READS `latest.json` but never recomposes a delivered brief, so the compose-time stamp never reached the live sidecars: `~/.mission-control/morning-brief/latest.json` and `~/.mission-control/data/brief.json` (both `generated_epoch=1783742596`) carried `valid_until=null`, and `dashboard status` reported today's brief `stale` with exit 1 all day despite the same-local-day validity requirement.
- Reasoning: The fix belongs at the compose defer point, not the dashboard reader, because a daily product's validity horizon is compose-time metadata (a fresh compose already stamps `valid_until` at line 879, before any delivery). When a compose run encounters a same-day *delivered* brief lacking `valid_until`, backfill it in place from the brief's OWN `generated_epoch` (next local midnight — same-local-day semantics), with no re-send and delivery idempotency untouched. `latest.json` is morning-brief's own sidecar; `data/brief.json` is a derived cache the dashboard's next `collect` re-builds from `latest.json` via the existing `_wrap` path (line 141-143), so migrating `latest.json` alone repairs both surfaces without morning-brief writing into the dashboard's DATA dir. Scoped to `delivery_state=="delivered"` (the audited case) to minimize blast radius into the delivery/deadman suites. `_render_markdown` does not render `valid_until`, so `markdown_sha256` is unaffected.
- Diagnosis inputs: Codex re-audit thread `019fd4d4-1f40-7a71-b52d-163057d646a5`; parent Fable governor handshake `5bffc31d`; `scripts/morning-brief` (`_compose_locked` defer branch, `_same_local_day`, `next_local_midnight`, `_atomic_write`); `scripts/dashboard` (`_wrap` line 130-147, `_run_builtin_brief` reader-not-composer at line 205-224, `cmd_status`, `_now`); `scripts/mission_control_common.py` (`feed_health` line 334-378, `next_local_midnight`); the live sidecars as ground truth (`brief_id 20260710-e0b7a9ca4b16`, delivered 2/2, `valid_until=null`); a pre-work advisor consult that flagged the sole-red-feed honesty caveat.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`; `scripts/morning-brief` gained `_migrate_valid_until(existing)` (idempotent: skips a non-int/≤0 epoch and an already-stamped sidecar; else writes `valid_until=next_local_midnight(generated_epoch)` via `_atomic_write`), called from the `_compose_locked` defer branch only when `delivery_state=="delivered"`; `scripts/dashboard` `_now()` gained a `MISSION_CONTROL_NOW_EPOCH` test seam (mirrors `MORNING_BRIEF_NOW_EPOCH`, prod-inert) so the freshness-critical status test is deterministic; `scripts/dashboard.test.sh` `c23` (legacy no-`valid_until` delivered sidecar → PRE status rc≠0 proves it was stale → no-arg compose migrates → collect re-derives → status rc=0, asserting both sidecars stamped and delivery state untouched; anchored to local noon today for TZ/date-proof determinism, chats overridden with a raw payload so it re-stamps fresh); `scripts/morning-brief-deadman.test.sh` asserts the deadman marker records the `install_stamp` provenance fields (audit's non-blocking caveat).
- Fix: landed the migration + regressions as commit `22cb92f`, pushed the feature branch, ran the migration once against live state (no-arg `morning-brief` → `migrated valid_until on 20260710-e0b7a9ca4b16` then `compose deferred while delivery state is delivered` — delivery 2/2 untouched), rebuilt `data/brief.json` via `dashboard collect --force brief`, and reinstalled the runtime `bin/` from `22cb92f` with the install-stamp updated.
- Test Evidence / Self-audit:
  - method: cold-reran all five required suites after edits; proved `c23` meaningful by asserting the PRE-migration legacy brief is stale (status rc≠0) before the migration step; ran the live migration and captured `dashboard status` before/after; reinstalled from HEAD and hash-compared installed `bin/` runtimes against `git show HEAD:scripts/<f>`.
  - outcome: `mission-control-common` PASS=1 FAIL=0; `morning-brief` ALL PASS; `morning-brief-delivery` ALL PASS; `morning-brief-deadman` ALL PASS (incl. new `install_stamp` assertion); `dashboard --require-shell` PASS=34 FAIL=0 (incl. new `c23`) — all cold. Live: BEFORE `brief 9299s ok stale` (EXIT 1); ran `morning-brief` → migrated `valid_until=1783753200` (next local midnight), delivery unchanged; AFTER `brief 9707s ok fresh`. Both sidecars now carry `valid_until=1783753200`. Installed `bin/morning-brief` sha256 `bc2271e1b2cc…` == HEAD, `morning-brief-deadman 816ba32c83c6…` == HEAD; `install-stamp.json` provenance `head`, head_sha `22cb92ff04fb`, 5 files; status install row `install 22cb92ff04fb ok verified (head)`.
  - honest caveat (advisor #1): live `dashboard status` overall EXIT is still 1 both before AND after — NOT from the brief, which flipped `stale`→`fresh`, but from two pre-existing unrelated live conditions present in the before snapshot too: `automation` has a red launchd job (`fresh (red job)`) and `chats` has a stale full transcript ingest (`fresh (stale full ingest)`). ### 2026-07-10 — Morning Brief round-2 trust defects (audit 019f4f6c reconciliation, Phase 0 item 1)
- Problem: The Codex audit `019f4f6c` reconciliation record (`global-implementations/records/verification/2026-07-10-audit-019f4f6c-orchestration-review.md`) found three brief trust defects still live in the installed runtime after fix round 1, plus an install-provenance gap: (a) the brief's `_input_health` read only the envelope `generated_epoch`, so it could report the chats input fresh while the last full transcript ingest was ~23h stale (the dashboard already surfaced this, the brief did not); (b) the brief feed carried only a 300s poll cadence, so `dashboard status` flagged a same-day brief `stale` (rc=1) after ~10 minutes and `index.html` desaturated it after ~2x cadence — a daily product judged on a poll clock; (c) `_open_changes` labeled every `change_type=="resolved"` row a plain "Resolved" regardless of `resolution_evidence_type`, so a `fork_dedup` consolidation and a `source_absent` inference both read as an evidence-backed resolution; and (Also, finding 2) the install step copied runtimes from the working tree, so a post-install fix that failed review would leave the runtime drifted from any committed SHA.
- Reasoning: Kill the envelope-vs-nested staleness class at the root by extracting ONE shared `feed_health()` into `mission_control_common.py` that folds in nested full-ingest staleness AND a daily product's `valid_until` window, then make the dashboard's `_verdict`/`_chats_stale_ingest` and the brief's `_input_health` thin adapters over it so no caller can forget a class again. Separate product validity from poll cadence by stamping `valid_until` (next local midnight) in the brief sidecar and honoring it in both status and the JS guard. Reserve "Resolved" for evidence-backed `resolution_evidence_type` values and mark the rest honestly. Make install source from the committed HEAD SHA and stamp a content hash + head SHA that status and the deadman re-verify without needing git. Same-local-day validity is shared with the deadman so a brief cannot be valid to one surface and stale to another. Deadman's `_send`/mobile-connect path (design D10) was deliberately untouched.
- Diagnosis inputs: the reconciliation record above (Phase 0 item 1 + finding 2); `scripts/morning-brief` (`_input_health`/`_open_changes`/`_compose_locked`/`_same_local_day`); `scripts/dashboard` (`_wrap`/`_verdict`/`_chats_stale_ingest`/`cmd_status`/`do_install`); `dashboard/index.html` per-feed `guard()`; `scripts/chat-graph` for the `resolution_evidence_type` enum (`manual`, `same_session_explicit`, `downstream_explicit`, `downstream`, `legacy` = evidence-backed; `fork_dedup`, `source_absent` = not); the five test suites for red-before-green shaping.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`; `scripts/mission_control_common.py` gained `feed_health`, `nested_ingest_stale`, `same_local_day`, `next_local_midnight`, `write_install_stamp`, `verify_install_stamp`; `scripts/dashboard` `_verdict`/`_chats_stale_ingest` delegate to `feed_health`, `_wrap` copies `valid_until` into the brief envelope, `cmd_status` prints an `install` verify row (red on drift), `do_install` sources from `git show HEAD:scripts/<f>` (worktree fallback only when `REPO_ROOT` is not a git repo) and stamps `bin/install-stamp.json`; `scripts/morning-brief` `_input_health` delegates to `feed_health` (1x cadence, no aging, nested_stale ⇒ stale), `_open_changes` keys off a new `_resolution_prefix`, `_compose_locked` stamps `valid_until`, and `_same_local_day` aliases the shared helper; `scripts/morning-brief-deadman` consumes the shared `same_local_day` and records the install-stamp verdict in `deadman-last-check.json`; `dashboard/index.html` `guard()` treats a feed as fresh while `now < valid_until`; regressions added to `scripts/morning-brief.test.sh` (nested freshness + evidence-honest wording) and `scripts/dashboard.test.sh` (`c20` daily-brief validity in status, `c21` committed-HEAD provenance + drift detection).
- Fix: landed all three trust defects + the install-provenance stamp as commit `ed95cce`, pushed the feature branch, reinstalled the runtime from that exact SHA with launchd untouched, and hash-verified every installed runtime against the committed HEAD.
- Self-audit:
  - method: proved each new regression RED against the committed pre-fix code before implementing (brief suite showed 2 FAILED; the committed dashboard printed `brief 7200s ok stale` for a same-day brief and wrote no `install-stamp.json`), then GREEN after; cold-reran all five suites; reinstalled with `DASHBOARD_INSTALL_NO_LAUNCHD=1` from HEAD and compared `shasum -a256` of each installed `bin/` runtime against `git show HEAD:scripts/<f>` (dashboard compared HEAD+baked-REPO_ROOT); ran the live `dashboard status` and the deadman once to confirm the stamp verifies and no alert fired.
  - outcome: `mission-control-common` PASS=1 FAIL=0; `morning-brief` ALL PASS (incl. 2 new); `morning-brief-delivery` ALL PASS; `morning-brief-deadman` ALL PASS; `dashboard --require-shell` PASS=32 FAIL=0 (incl. `c20`, `c21`) — all cold. Installed `bin/` hashes all matched HEAD `ed95cce` (`morning-brief fc9d9200…`, `deadman 816ba32c…`, `common 95f5f755…`, `decision-alert d418936e…`, `dashboard 7a8c7c3b…`). `install-stamp.json`: provenance `head`, head_sha `ed95cce598149c…`, 5 files. `dashboard status` install row: `install ed95cce59814 ok verified (head)`. Deadman `deadman-last-check.json`: `install_stamp {present:true, ok:true, head_sha ed95cce…, provenance head}`, `result healthy`, `alerted false` (today's brief `20260710-e0b7a9ca4b16` delivered 2/2, same local day — no notification sent).
  - did not verify: the single Codex re-audit covering round 1 (`2c9d1a1`) + round 2 (`ed95cce`) — still owed before merge; the natural 7:00 AM fire on real cadence; the five-morning comprehension proof. The deadman mobile-connect independence (design D10) was intentionally not touched (separate queued item). No merge to main.

The deadman judges brief *delivery* from `latest.json`, not feed envelopes, so it has no feed to health-check — it shares the validity helper (`same_local_day`) and the install-stamp verifier instead, which is the real duplication the plan targeted. ### 2026-07-10 — Morning Brief render defect fixes (Codex audit of 0312a3a: P1 privacy + P1 comprehension + two P2 provenance)
- Problem: A Codex audit (thread `019f4f5f-6917-7331-a4e6-d8b2e7e9da67`) of activation commit `0312a3a` found four render-lane defects, two verified with synthetic behavioral proofs: (P1 privacy) `_needs_you` clipped decision text with `_headline` BEFORE `_sanitize_line` ran the shared egress policy, so a sensitive line whose secret/email sat past the clip boundary survived as a partial, "safe"-looking headline; (P1 comprehension) the live `latest.md` NEEDS YOU block ran 5 decision rows + 5 action rows + a collapse line (~1.4 KB the audit measured, 5,383 bytes on the 2026-07-10 live brief), failing the one-phone-screen bar; (P2) section dedup keyed only on `(text, action_cmd)`, so rows with different trust/source/depth could silently collapse; (P2) markdown rendered `trust` but never the sidecar `source`.
- Reasoning: These are render-path-only defects. The smallest correct slice sanitizes the RAW line before any transform (privacy floor: a clip must never rescue a row the egress policy drops), narrows NEEDS YOU by top-N significance selection with character clipping only (never word/line counts), widens the dedup identity, and adds source to the line render — no composer, delivery, or feed logic touched.
- Diagnosis inputs: Codex audit thread `019f4f5f-6917-7331-a4e6-d8b2e7e9da67` with per-defect synthetic proofs; the parent Fable governor handshake `941a7827`; `scripts/morning-brief` (`_sanitize_line`/`_headline`/`_needs_you`/`_section`/`_render_markdown`/`_compose_locked`); `scripts/mission_control_common.py` egress contract; the live `~/.mission-control/morning-brief/latest.md` (5,383 bytes, 5 NEEDS YOU decisions) as ground truth.
- Implementation inputs: branch `feature/morning-brief-activation`; in `scripts/morning-brief` — `_sanitize_line` gained a `clip=` arg and now sanitizes raw text FIRST then applies `_headline` only to survivors; `_needs_you` passes `clip=chars` instead of pre-clipping, and filters egress-dropped (`None`) rows before the empty check so a feed of only-sensitive candidates still shows the reassuring placeholder and an accurate collapsed count; the NEEDS YOU default cap dropped from 5 to 3 (`MORNING_BRIEF_NEEDS_YOU_MAX`); `_section` dedup key widened to `(text, action_cmd, trust, source, depth)`; `_render_markdown` now renders `_(source)_` per line and the collapse line reads `+K more: dashboard`; two new regression tests in `scripts/morning-brief.test.sh` (a compose-level fail-closed proof that a past-boundary secret drops the whole NEEDS YOU row, and a unit invariant that `clip` never rescues a dropped row).
- Fix: repaired all four audited defects in the render lane, added the required fail-closed regression, reinstalled the runtime `bin/` copy so the 7:00 AM agent runs the fixed code, and recomposed safely (idempotency deferred because today's brief was already delivered — no duplicate Telegram send).
- Self-audit:
  - method: cold-reran the three required suites after edits plus the two new regressions; ran a standalone synthetic proof contrasting the old clip-before-sanitize path (leaks a shortened safe headline) against the new sanitize-before-clip path (drops the row); `diff`+`shasum` on the installed runtime bin vs the repo script; ran a real `morning-brief` compose to confirm idempotency deferral; a read-only `--print` preview to eyeball the fixed NEEDS YOU (3 rows + `+11 more: dashboard` + per-line source).
  - outcome: `bash scripts/morning-brief.test.sh` (10 checks incl. 2 new), `bash scripts/morning-brief-delivery.test.sh`, and `bash scripts/morning-brief-deadman.test.sh` all `ALL PASS` cold; synthetic proof printed old→`'Pick the deploy window … (options in dashboard)'` vs new→`None`; installed `~/.mission-control/bin/morning-brief` SHA `6580840a8c7da46f...` == repo script; `morning-brief` returned `compose deferred while delivery state is delivered: 20260710-e0b7a9ca4b16` (delivery state unchanged, 2/2 chunks); `--print` showed NEEDS YOU narrowed to 3 rows with source suffixes and no clipped-prefix leak.
  - did not verify: the natural 7:00 AM fire on real cadence (next scheduled run); the five-morning comprehension proof; the notification-consolidation decision. Today's already-delivered `latest.md` was intentionally NOT overwritten (idempotency) — the fixed rendering first reaches a delivered artifact at the next fresh compose.

### 2026-07-04 — Map recent chat journal
- Problem: Trevor wanted the connected-chat view to show a tiny next-day memory aid: what he touched, when, and which chat it was.

  - outcome: passed; screenshot shows the five-row journal above the graph.

- by: Codex thread `019f2b73-9811-7392-b511-201c1f109997`.

## Suggested Recommendation Log
If it's not here, it isn't remembered.
Keep materially new suggestions here so they survive beyond the current chat.
- Forward entry shape should include `date`, `recommendation`, `why`, `by:`, and `linear:`.
- Do not delete old entries; mark them completed, declined, deferred, or superseded with date and chat context.
- Keep audit-created items here only when they are deferred, optional, or not yet execution-ready; otherwise promote them into `## Active Next Steps`.
- When a suggestion comes from an audit or feedback review, link back to the originating audit record or `Feedback Decision Log` entry and later note which chat implemented or declined it.
- 2026-07-12 | recommendation: accepted compatibility debt — migrate `mc-panel.swift` from deprecated `NSUserNotification` APIs to `UserNotifications` only when a supported macOS SDK removes the current API, notifications fail in live use, or the panel already needs a permission-flow redesign. | why: the current binary compiles and runs, while migration would add notification authorization behavior and is not a correctness fix for this local menu-bar surface. | by: Codex thread `019f59f8-bb9e-70c0-9497-a9686ea24154`, from independent UX/test audit. | linear: repo-only; no live Mission Control Linear team is configured.
- 2026-07-11 | recommendation: design an inflight/reconciliation state machine before claiming exactly-once or provider-idempotent Morning Brief chunk delivery. | why: the current delivery contract is at-least-once; if a provider accepts a chunk and the local process crashes before recording the receipt, the retry path must treat it as ambiguous and may resend. The queued state machine should track provider-accepted/local-unconfirmed chunks and reconcile them without changing the current sender in round 8. | by: Codex thread `019f5056-bc55-7043-88ad-b87b1ac48a6f`, governed by Claude `e85411a1-b74b-4556-9939-8eaf5d8f2ea9`. | linear: repo-only; no Mission Control Linear team is configured.
- 2026-07-04 | recommendation: pattern-mine the dashboard/coding-tracker search audit during the next ER-089/UI polish pass: provider reset/pace cards, usage attribution tables, active-session state list, source-confidence labels, and a later git-yield lens; do not rewrite Mission Control into Next/React, a remote-control platform, or a generic drag/drop dashboard. | why: the highest-fit repos confirm Mission Control's local/static architecture but reveal better visual and workflow affordances for usage, sessions, and attention routing. | by: Codex thread `019f2dec-d36b-7093-8259-8d9df30dede0`; record: `records/2026-07-04-dashboard-coding-tracker-search-audit.md`. | linear: self-contained until Linear is configured.
- 2026-07-04 | recommendation: pattern-mine `tabler/tabler` during the next Mission Control UI polish pass, especially cards, dense tables, badges, tabs, segmented controls, and offline-safe icon affordances; do not install `@tabler/core` wholesale unless a later implementation proves the static file:// bundle stays simpler and safer. | why: Tabler is a mature MIT Bootstrap dashboard kit, but this repo's product intent is still a single-file local/offline dashboard. | by: Codex thread `019f2d4e-54ba-7273-8188-12506a3daf19`; source card: third-party index `researched-repos/tabler-tabler.md` commit `171a122`. | linear: self-contained until Linear is configured.
- 2026-07-05 | recommendation: do not adopt the GitHub Copilot enterprise-observability stack (OpenTelemetry Collector, Prometheus, Grafana, OpenObserve, Superset, Metabase, Airbyte, Meltano, dbt-core, Great Expectations, TensorZero, Helicone, OpenLIT, traceAI, TraceRoot, Pull Request Analytics Action); treat `records/2026-07-04-dashboard-coding-tracker-search-audit.md` as the real same-niche repo map; if a chart is ever justified, prefer vendorable zero-dependency `leeoniya/uPlot` over Chart.js/ECharts/CDN — but not for V1. | why: Copilot recommended from the repo description alone (it said so); every headline pick runs as a background service, framework, or separate warehouse and collides with the explicit non-goals of offline single-file, single-user, no-server. Full evaluation in Feedback Decision Log 2026-07-05. | by: Claude Code (Opus 4.8) session `a9724039-6595-4205-a25b-bf361020250a`. | linear: self-contained until Linear is configured.

## Audit Record Convention
If it's not here, it isn't remembered.
- Record each audit, ship-check, or substantial verification-driven review in an easy-to-find project audit log entry.
- Each entry should capture:
  - `date`
  - `type` (for example `full audit`, `targeted audit`, `ship-check`, `governance review`)
  - `scope`
  - `repo fingerprint` (branch + commit when available)
  - `prior audit reference`
  - `source/work chat`
  - `audit chat`
  - `implementation chat` or `disposition chat`
  - `separate follow-up audit` (`yes` / `no` plus reason when `no`)
  - `commands / evidence`
  - `tested`
  - `not tested`
  - `findings opened or updated`
  - `fixes closed / verified`
  - `declined / deferred findings`
  - `better-path challenge`
  - `references` (issue, PR, commit, or log path)
  - `by`
  - `linear`
- When a finding is later implemented, deferred, declined, or superseded, update the existing audit trail instead of deleting the history.

## Test Evidence Convention
If it's not here, it isn't remembered.
- Testing is required delivery evidence. If a check is skipped, blocked, or only partially run, record the reason and the remaining risk.
- Record each verification run as:
  - `date` (YYYY-MM-DD)
  - `command(s)` executed
  - `result` (pass/fail + short note)
  - `log/PR reference` (commit SHA, CI URL, or local log path)
  - `by`
  - `linear`
- When a verification run closes or updates an audit finding, cross-reference the matching audit record entry and the chat or commit that performed the work.

## Testing Cadence Matrix
| Trigger | Command(s) | Cadence | Gate Criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chat graph logic change | `bash scripts/chat-graph.test.sh` | Per change | Tests pass and export/doctor behavior is covered |
| Automation status or job registry change | `bash scripts/automation-status.test.sh` | Per change | Tests pass and registry states remain honest |
| Dashboard collector/install/status change | `REPO_ROOT="$PWD" bash scripts/dashboard.test.sh --require-shell` | Per change | Shell, fixtures, status, install, and render smoke pass |
| Dashboard renderer or fixture change | `node scripts/dashboard-render-smoke.js .` | Per change | Every tab renders fixture content; Map builds graph elements |
| Any non-trivial release or merge closeout | `scripts/verify.sh` | Per closeout | Every committed suite, real-browser gate, scanner self-test, strict OpenSpec, and syntax check passes |
| Usage snapshot logic change | `bash scripts/usage-snapshot.test.sh` | Per change | Credit notification command handling remains argv-only and advice is delivered as one message |
| Release-sensitive visual/file:// change | `node scripts/dashboard-browser.test.js` plus a capture of installed or demo output | Per change | All seven tabs are console-clean and nonblank at desktop/mobile widths; no document overflow; Map canvas, keyboard controls, contrast, and truthful clipboard failure pass |

- 2026-07-05 | feedback source: Trevor pasted a GitHub Copilot third-party-repo recommendation for this repo and asked which suggestions to agree with and which could actually be implemented | feedback summary: Copilot — working only from the repo description, not its contents, which it openly admitted — recommended an enterprise observability + data-warehouse + business-intelligence stack (OpenTelemetry Collector, Prometheus, Grafana, OpenObserve, Superset, Metabase, Airbyte, Meltano, dbt-core, Great Expectations, TensorZero, Helicone, OpenLIT, traceAI, TraceRoot, a Pull Request Analytics GitHub Action, onWatch, ECharts/Chart.js) plus a 4-phase "adopt Collector+Prometheus+Grafana+OpenLIT+dbt" plan | evaluation chat: this session (full adversarial review of pasted AI output) | reasoning response: category error against PROJECT_INTENT. One test disposes of the headline list — does it run as a background service/framework/separate warehouse, or is it a vendorable file the page loads offline? OTel Collector, Prometheus, Grafana, OpenObserve, Superset, Metabase, Airbyte, Meltano, dbt-core, Great Expectations, and TensorZero all fail it and collide with the explicit non-goals (no server, no framework rebuild, single-user, offline/file://). Copilot's Phase-1 (Collector+Prometheus+Grafana) would replace the double-clickable offline file with a multi-service ops stack — a different product, not a heavier version of this one. LLM-observability picks (Helicone/OpenLIT/traceAI/TraceRoot) require SDK/proxy instrumentation that cannot attach to first-party Claude Code/Codex/Cursor and reintroduce the rejected proxy/cookie surface. TensorZero is an LLM gateway/router, violating the passive read-only design. Great Expectations duplicates existing feed-boundary validation + `dashboard-render-smoke` at far heavier scale. What actually transfers is only: (a) the category instinct that token/session/cost observability is the right neighborhood — already better mapped by the 2026-07-04 dashboard-coding-tracker audit; (b) vendor-neutral normalized ingestion — already implemented as feed envelopes; (c) "cost per merged PR"-style metrics — already queued as the V2 yield lens; none require new infrastructure. onWatch was already triaged in the prior audit (GPL, pattern-only); no other Copilot pick improves on that audit's peer set. `leeoniya/uPlot` (tiny zero-dependency MIT canvas charting) is noted only as the right-sized counter-example to Copilot's Chart.js/ECharts/CDN pick if a chart ever earns its place; it still fails this repo's "no new dependency unless it reduces local-operability risk" bar for V1 | decision status: rejected (adopt none of the recommended tools) with partial value captured — category instinct and two ideas already reflected in the ER-089 usage-routing and V2 yield-lens queue | implementation/disposition chat: this session; nothing adopted, installed, or wired | linked branch / audit / suggestion / test evidence: `records/2026-07-04-dashboard-coding-tracker-search-audit.md` (the correct same-niche map); Suggested Recommendation Log 2026-07-05 | by: Claude Code (Opus 4.8) session `a9724039-6595-4205-a25b-bf361020250a` | linear: self-contained until Linear is configured.
